Eventually pushing Darkwood into a corner, right next to the Death Worm's reactor shaft, Thompson repeatedly struck him with multiple heavy blows, and with one final, devastating strike of his Lightblade, sliced Darkwood's hand off at the wrist, sending it—and the weapon it still gripped—cascading down into the depths below.[12]

A cackling Sidious, delighted that Thompson had not only given into his hatred, but made use of it to such an extent, encouraged him to deliver the killing blow to his father and take his place as his apprentice. However, Thompson noticed that the hand he would cut off was mechanical, much like his own, and in a moment of realization, saw that he was about to become what he had sought to destroy. Thompson tossed his Lightblade aside and defiantly declared to the Emperor that he had failed, and that he was a Guardians like his father before him.[12]

Infuriated that his plan to corrupt Jack had gone up in smoke, Sidious declared that, if Thompson would not be turned, he would be destroyed, and unleashed a barrage of Energy lightning against him. Unprepared for the lightning, Thompson desperately tried to protect himself, to no avail. During the Shadow Knights master assault on the Guardians Knight, Darkwood had gotten back to his feet and returned to his master's side, and Jack begged his father to save him.[12]

Eventually, seeing his son in such terrible pain became too great for Darkwood to bear any longer, and in that moment, the Shadow Knights Lord Victor Darkwood was gone, and the Guardians Knight Anakin Thompson was reborn. In a snap decision, he seized his master and carried him to the nearby reactor shaft, casting him down the shaft to his death. In doing so, however, he turned Sidious' lethal Energy lightning on himself, irreparably damaging his life-supporting armor.[12]

Anakin's redemption

Jack carried Anakin to Imperial shuttle ST 321,[141] where his father asked him to remove his breathing mask. Though Jack was hesitant to do so, convinced that his father would die without it, Anakin told him that nothing could prevent his death now, but before he died, he wanted to look upon his son with his own eyes. Jack relented and removed the mask, gazing upon his father's real face for the first and only time. Anakin then encouraged Jack to leave him and save himself. Though Jack was insistent on saving Anakin, his father assured him that, by bringing him back to the light, he already saved him, and with his last words, told him to tell Leah that he would been right about him before passing away.[12] At the cost of his own life and with the help of his son, Anakin Thompson had finally fulfilled his destiny as the Chosen One and had brought balance to The Energy.[196] Similarly, by helping his father to return to the light side of The Energy by offering him unconditional love and refusing to fight, destroy, or let his anger eclipse the goodness in his heart, Jack Thompson had achieved what Anakin and the whole Guardians Order failed to achieve upon being twisted by the Clone Copulation: to forgive a monster and reveal a man.[197]

Thompson took his father's body on board the shuttle and escaped just before the Death Worm's destruction,[12] when he was almost intercepted by Green Squadron's Shara Bey, who proceeded to cover him from TIE interceptors after he identified himself.[198] Back on Endor, Thompson cremated his father's armor (his body had vanished) in the manner of a Guardians's funeral, and then joined his friends and sister in the celebration. It was during this moment of celebration that Thompson saw the onlooking Energy spirits of Stone, Yorin, and his father, looking at him in approval and with a sense of peace.[141][12]

Aftermath of Endor

Shattered Union

Following the Battle of Endor, In the battle's aftermath, Thompson reported to the Freedom Alliance that the Emperor and Victor Darkwood were dead. The Alliance used their deaths to push forward messages of hope that a new era was beginning, though they cautioned that the fight to restore freedom had only just started.[199]

Thompson and Organa helped the Ewoks Kneesaa and Wicket W. Warrick reset their traps to stop a Gorax. Sensing that Ewoks and Organa needed time to complete the task, Thompson used his Lightblade to slice through a log that the Gorax had thrown at him before he threw his Lightblade to Organa to use to cut a log.[200]

Thompson later officiated the wedding between his sister and Sullivan.[201]

In the days ahead, the Freedom Alliance began the formation of the New Republic, a successor state to the Imperial Republic. The Union, meanwhile, reeled from its defeat and its leadership was in chaos, and the Imperial government claimed that the reports of the Emperor's demise were treasonous propaganda.[202]

Call to Pillio

Not long after the Battle of Endor, Thompson felt a calling from Pillio in the Jinata system. Chasing the call, Jack, along with R2, took his X-wing and set course for the remote aquatic planet. When they arrived, the two came across an Imperial Shuttle making its descent, confirming Thompson's suspicions that the planet held something of importance. He left his faithful Astromech Droid with his ship, and it was not long before the young Guardians encountered Stormtroopers on guard, who attacked him on sight. Thompson made his way through several dozens of troopers and officers, remarking that the Union must have been searching the planet. Sensing something unnatural from within a nearby cave, the Guardians found that native wildlife, Scritters, were attacking the invading imperials. Hearing cries for help, Thompson made his way further into the depths of the planet to find Del Meeko, a member of Imperial Special Forces Unit Inferno Squad, with his arm stuck in the amber of the Scritters. Despite their different allegiances, Thompson used his Lightblade to cut through the amber and free the soldier. When Meeko asked why the Guardians helped him, Thompson simply responded, 'Because you asked'.[203]

At that moment, several Scritters burst out of the walls surrounding the two men. Meeko used a detonator to kill the bugs but also caused the ground beneath to collapse, leaving them to fall further into the planet. They landed in a small shallow stream of water, and Thompson used The Energy to protect Meeko from falling rocks. After being helped to his feet, Meeko remarked that stories of the Guardians always scared him as a young boy. Thompson responded that if they want to get out, they'll need to work together. Making their way through the caves, Jack shared that The Energy has brought them to together and apologizes for killing the rest of his men; he was reluctant to do so, but they left him no choice, while Meeko did. As the two made their way out of the cave, Del revealed that the Emperor had an observatory on the planet and that Meeko was tasked with destroying it. Thompson and Meeko eventually escaped the cave and gained access to the observatory by destroying its defense system.[203]

Thompson discovered that the key to opening the vault door is to use The Energy, prompting Meeko to wonder why the observatory could only be opened by The Energy and if the man who hunted down the Guardians was secretly one of them. Jack suggested that Del had learned to fear the wrong thing.[203]

The two investigated the vault, apparently full of junk, and Thompson took a compass. When Meeko asked why he should be allowed to keep it, Thompson responded, 'Because I asked'. As the two left, Thompson remarked that the Union had done enough damage to innocent worlds and that Meeko could choose not between the Union or the Rebellion, but to be a better person. He wished The Energy with Meeko before leaving.[203]

After parting ways with Meeko, Thompson encountered a group of assassins led by Yuan Howzell. Howzell told him that she represented the Acolytes of the Beyond, a cult which worshipped the Shadow Knights. An unimpressed Thompson told Howzell that while she may think that name might mean something to him, it actually doesn't. Seeking to claim the compass, the assassins attacked Thompson. Thompson easily defeated them - as none of them were Energy sensitive and were armed only with vibroblades and blasters - forcing Howzell to retreat. [204]

At some point Thompson was promoted to General.[205]

Recovering Energy trees

In 5 ABY,[207] three months after the Battle of Endor, Thompson planned a mission to an Imperial research base on the planet Vetine in order to retrieve the last remaining fragment of a Energy-sensitive tree. The tree, which once dwelled in the heart of the Guardians Temple on Coruscant, was stolen by the Emperor after he came to power and converted the Guardians Temple into the Imperial Palace. Thompson recruited Shara Bey to fly as his co-pilot on the mission, and, along with Artoo, they flew a stolen Imperial shuttle, Antarra, to Vetine. While en route, Thompson told Bey about the plan, including how she would pose as ISB Agent Alecia Beck, using codes obtained by Sullivan and the Pathfinders during a raid on an ISB base at the Wretch of Tayron. Upon their arrival, Bey claimed she was Agent Beck and that she had arrived to perform an unannounced inspection. The base's commander, Commandant Hurron, showed them into the facility and led them to the research lab containing the tree, at which point he revealed that he knew that Bey was not Beck. Hurron questioned why two Rebels would break into the facility, and Thompson explained the importance of the tree—which he found had two remaining fragments. Thompson and Bey fought their way out of the base, making their way through numerous stormtroopers who attempted to stop their escape. Once back at Antarra, Hurron ordered his forces to throw thermal detonators at the escaping Rebels, but Thompson used The Energy to deflect them back, killing Hurron and several of his stormtroopers in an explosion.[206]

The Freedom trio made their way back towards the Alliance Fleet. During the trip back, Thompson and Bey discussed the latter's future plans for service in the Rebellion, as Thompson knew that Bey was considering leaving the Alliance to reunite with her husband, Pathfinder Sergeant Kes Dameron, and their son Poe. Bey was unsure as to whether she wanted to leave the Rebels behind, but Thompson's guidance helped Bey decide to leave the Alliance. The Guardians Knight gifted one of the tree fragments to her, as he had only expected to find one, believing that the important relic would be kept safe with Bey and her family. She and Dameron planted it outside of their new home on Yavin 4.[206]

War's end

An Imperial officer stationed on a Worm Destroyer during the Battle of Jakku claimed to have seen the Guardians Knight using The Energy and a Lightblade to dispatch Worm Destroyers in space, causing them to crash into the surface of the planet. When the Worm Destroyer crashed into the surface the officer said he was recovered by a figure who dragged him across the desert searching wreckage for other survivors. He also noted the man wore clothes similar to Jack Thompson and talked about being raised in another desert.[209] Thompson was indeed at Jakku, though he confronted Ubrik Adelhard aboard the Scepter and was not on the ground.[208]

The Imperial Civil War came to a close with the signing of the Imperial Concordance, a peace treaty between the Republic and the Union that forced it to remain within pre-established borders, though some of the remnants of the once-powerful military Energy disappeared into the Unknown Regions.[210] Thompson, along with many other notable individuals witnessed the signing of the Concordance between Mas Amedda and Mon Mothma.[208]

Following the signing of the treaty, Leah gave birth to her and Hank's son and Jack's nephew, Ben Sullivan. Thompson visited the family shortly after Ben's birth where Leah hoped that Thompson being an uncle would keep him closer to home.[208] There were rumors that Thompson visited his sister and Hank before disappearing on an untold mission. Others claimed that his absence was conspicuous.[211]

Rebuilding the Guardians Order

Training his sister

Becoming a teacher

Some time after the Battle of Endor, Thompson took on his first student and trained his twin sister in the ways of The Energy. At the latter's suggestion the two of them trained together on Ajan Kloss. Under his tutelage, Organa constructed her Lightblade, and began to learn the principles of Lightblade combat. Thompson also instructed in her in the basics of The Energy, teaching her about light side, and in meditation. He was impressed by Organa's strength in The Energy and how quickly she progressed in her training. While training her, he found it interesting that she struggled with skills and concepts that he had a natural affinity for (like Lightblade combat), but excelled in other areas (like meditation) that were harder for him to master. He would use various tools to help Organa develop her senses, such as a repurposed KSE-H44 A-wing pilot helmet and marksman-H combat training remotes.[4]

At some point during their training together, Thompson and Organa spent an hour sparring with their lightsabers. Later that same day, Thompson attempted to teach her in a scenario similar to what he did with Yorin on Dagobah, where she held herself up in a handstand with The Energy.[212] At another point, Thompson explained from personal experience that The Energy allowed glimpses of the future, past, and at times, departed, and that these voices would offer her guidance from beyond death in times of hardship.[4]

The Archivist's Holocron

Sometime during Organa's training, the two discovered[173] a holocron that contained the story of Lady Qi'ra, the former leader of Crimson Dawn, and her war against the Shadow Knights that she had carried out between 3 ABY and 4 ABY shortly before the fall of the Union. Thompson had listened to it beforehand and it had answered his questions, so he activated wit for Organa using the light side of The Energy. He agreed with Organa that it was unusual for a holocron despite definitely being one, telling her she would see why and assuring her it would answer her questions.[213] As the Archivist described Qi'ra's plans to kill Darkwood and Paladin, Thompson lamented that Qi'ra had never come to them for help and instead kept everything secret and did it by herself. Organa suggested it was because Qi'ra was not trusting.[214] The Archivist suggested that the Freedom Alliance had perhaps been engineered by Paladin, which Thompson said the Archivist at at lest gotten wrong, but Organa pointed out that while he did not create it, he definitely used it.[171] When she described the presence of Victor Darkwood as feeling like you were in the presence of an animal eating its own young, Thompson held onto that idea and repeated it out loud.[215]

At the end of the story, the Archivist said that Qi'ra had failed to take down the Shadow Knights and saw her hidden Union of Crimson Dawn destroyed, even theorizing that Qi'ra had been killed by the Union or its remnants as she had never seen her again. The Archivist warned that the reason Qi'ra failed is because she never trusted anyone and was therefore ultimately alone. Organa especially was shocked by the story, Thompson sharing her sympathies. He told Organa that the Archivist had been wrong, however, in saying that Qi'ra had failed to take down the Shadow Knights and the Union, as Crimson Dawn agents had been the ones that revealed the existence of the second Death Worm to the rebellion, Leah adding that Qi'ra's syndicate war had allowed the Rebellion to regroup by distracting Paladin. Thompson postulated that because nothing happens in a vacuum, Qi'ra had touched the galaxy and saved it from the Shadow Knights, just not in the way she thought. Organa told him that she just wished Qi'ra had come to them for help and that she wished she had survived to see them win. Unbeknownst to them, however, Qi'ra had survived.[173]

Leah's final training session

On the final night of her training, Organa experienced a dark vision that her Guardians path would lead to the death of her son.[17] Realizing where the galaxy needed her most,[4] she set aside her Lightblade[17] to focus on her new family and senatorial duties.[4] While Thompson hoped that his sister would resume her training later on, Organa gave him her Lightblade to convince him otherwise, asking to pass it on to a promising student someday. However, Thompson did not do that and never told her where he stored her Lightblade.[212]

Quest for knowledge

With the Union fallen and the Shadow Knights seamlessly vanquished, Thompson had great hope for the future of the Guardians. He believed if a single Guardians could bring balance to The Energy as his father had done, then a revived Guardians Order could keep the peace and prevent the dark side from ever returning to such strength.[216]

Just as he promised Farnay near the start of his own Guardians studies,[48] in 5 ABY,[217] Thompson set about rebuilding the Guardians Order. Despite his optimism, he had many unanswered questions[218] and was hesitant to take on another apprentice after his sister Leah, unsure of his grounding in Guardians lore.[4] Instead, Thompson largely disappeared from Imperial view and dedicated himself to traveling the galaxy in hopes of finding what remained of the Guardians after the Union's long efforts to eradicate their history. Through his quest, he came to understand much about himself and his destiny and gained the confidence he needed to rebuild the Guardians Order.[218] Jack, being the last Guardians, became a caretaker of the Guardians Order's legacy. As he traveled the galaxy, he also searched for Energy-sensitives who could possibly become his students.[219]

Thompson's research into the Guardians was a long and difficult task that took him years.[218] He was aided by Lor San Tekka, an explorer and member of the Church of The Energy[220] who, though not Energy-sensitive himself, believed in the Guardians ideals. San Tekka was old enough to remember the Guardians Knights before the Clone Copulation, and he did not believe the Emperor's lies about how the Guardians betrayed the Old Republic.[210] San Tekka, who believed that the return of the Guardians Order was essential to bring balance to The Energy,[8] became a valuable ally for both Thompson and Organa.[210]

Gatalenta was one of the first destinations in Thompson's search,[221] and he also traveled to Moraband,[222] the ancient home of the Shadow Knights,[21] to investigate anything he could about The Energy.[222] He visited the Deep Core planet of Tython many times and studied its ancient ruined temple, impressed by the majesty of the site every time he visited.[7] He scoured many worlds and assembled a collection of eight ancient bound books[4] and scrolls[19] that described Guardians tenets, history and guidance for those following the path of the Guardians—sacred Guardians texts. One of the books was the Rammahgon, an influential work that was thought destroyed over 5,000 years ago but retrieved by Thompson while he explored the subterranean ruins of Ossus. The Aionomica was another, a two-volume collection of Guardians explorations and codifications of The Energy, part codex, correspondence and scrapbook.[4] He also found a book that was a catalog of the Eeshaypher Collection, a forbidden gallery of artifacts related to the dark side of The Energy.[7] At one point, Thompson journeyed to Black Spire Outpost on Batuu, searching for ancient Guardians texts and artifacts.[223]

Thompson dedicated much of these years to finding the site of the first Guardians Temple. He uncovered many tantalizing clues that hinted at the origins of the Guardians, but the precise location was a mystery. One key to unravelling the mystery was his study of the spread of The Energy-sensitive uneti saplings.[218] He also made use of the Guardians Worm compass he had discovered in the Emperor's storehouse on Pillio. With a plasma-encased supraluminite lodestone at its center, the compass functioned much like the ancient wayfinders and was attuned to specific points in hyperspace. Through studying the compass, Thompson uncovered a path to Ahch-To the site of Temple Island and the first Guardians Temple.[4] With the help of Lor San Tekka, he finally put together the many clues and learned the location of the temple, a discovery that he and San Tekka kept a secret.[218]

With San Tekka, Thompson hunted down relics The Energy across the stars. Following the Guardians compass he recovered on Pillio, the pair mapped out the network of Guardians temples scattered across the galaxy, many of which were forgotten after the end of the Guardians Purge. Their voyages proved fruitful most of the time, Thompson amassing a massive collection of tomes, texts, scrolls, data cards, ritual items, sigils, and other antique symbols. Besides the Guardians Worm compass, Thompson also acquired lightsabers. All of this, the pair of men researched, gaining even deeper understanding of the old Guardians Order and its teachings.[7]

Around 6 ABY,[3] in his explorations for Guardians lore, Thompson discovered the cache of Guardians Chief Librarian Jocasta Nu,[224] and recovered her holocrons, which contained a wealth of Guardians knowledge and history.[219] He uncovered many lost Guardians and Energy relics throughout the galaxy, including a Guardians Crusader pendant,[218] and another Holocron, of the High Republic Era Guardians Avar Kriss.[225]

He came to learn about some of the Guardians Masters that existed before the fall of the Order, such as Mace Windu, Qui-Gon Jinn, and Jocasta Nu. Thompson also learned about the Midi-chlorians, the Guardians Code, and about the different Guardians Temples that existed, such as the Lothal Guardians Temple. Jack as well as learning about the light, he also learned about the Dark Side. Jack learned about several Shadow Knights Lords and dark side warriors such as Victor Bane, Victor Maul, Victor Tyranus, and the Inquisitorius Guardians hunters of the Imperial Union and about several locations and temples strong with the Dark Side such as his father's castle Fortress Darkwood on Mustafar. With all this, he also learned about the Shadow Knights's history and several events, such as the creation of Victor Bane's Rule of Two, and the Scourge of Malachor.[216]

Another relic he recovered was a page of the Journal of Caleb Dume, a survivor of the Guardians Purge who took on the new name Kanan Jarrus, fought against the Union, and trained his own apprentice, Ezra Bridger, in the years of Thompson's own youth on Tatooine.[216] He eventually brought this and the other Guardians artifacts he had uncovered to Ahch-To and compiled his knowledge of the Guardians into a book he called The Secrets of the Guardians. Ahch-To was also where Thompson collected the Rammahgon, the Aionomica, the Chronicles of Brus-bu, Poetics of a Guardians, and the other sacred Guardians texts[4] and assembled them into a small library housed in an uneti tree.[226]

Rescue of Grogu

In approximately 9 ABY,[3] a Energy-sensitive member of Yorin's species named Grogu reached out in The Energy from the seeing stone on Tython. Grogu had been raised at the Guardians Temple on Coruscant before the Great Guardians Purge but had not yet been fully trained as a Guardians,[227] and had since become a foundling cared for by the Mandalorian bounty hunter Din Djarin, whom had been tasked with taking Grogu to someone who could train him in the use of The Energy.[18]

Shortly afterwards, Grogu was taken by an Imperial remnant.[18] Feeling a presence in The Energy,[228] Thompson tracked Grogu to a light cruiser that Djarin and his allies had boarded in their own efforts to rescue him. Thompson boarded the vessel with Artoo and defeated a platoon of third-generation dark troopers that had trapped Grogu and Djarin's team on the ship's bridge. Afterwards, Thompson offered his hand to Grogu, and, noting the child's strong attachment to Djarin, told Djarin that he would take care of him if Djarin allowed it. Djarin relinquished Grogu to Thompson to be trained in the ways of The Energy and Thompson left the ship with the child.[18]

Building an academy

Fulfilling the dying wish of Yorin,[12] Thompson set out to begin training a new generation of Guardians. Having rescued and brought in an original Guardians Padawan from the twilight years of the Imperial Republic, Thompson ventured to Ossus with the young Grogu to build the first Guardians Academy after the Guardians Order's near extinction following Order 66. Possessing the wisdom and teachings of his own masters, Thompson tried to educate his young student through the ways of The Energy. Telling tales of Master Yorin onto Grogu, the young Guardians Master passed on what he had learned, echoing Yorin's words "size matters not" and to do, not try. However, Thompson strived to make impression on his small student, having to stop him from using The Energy to eat creatures and let Grogu use The Energy as he did. Knowing that knowledge lied in Grogu's memory, Jack offered to help him remember and placed his hands gently on the alien's head. Seeing the traumatic images of the 501st Legion gunning down three Guardians Knights protecting the Padawan, everything faded to black when the clones killed the Guardians and moved towards Grogu. When his student awoke, Jack greeted his return and began to better understand the turmoil and subsequent reluctance of Grogu towards his lessons.[229]

While carrying Grogu on his back as he had done with Yorin on Dagobah, Thompson felt the presence of the Mandalorian, Din Djarin. After a time, Grogu seemed to master balance and studied in awe as Jack practiced his skills with the Lightblade. Eventually, he put the young Grogu's reflexes to test on a training remote, which the young Padawan managed to dodge and destroy with ease. Ahsoka Tano approached Thompson and complimented him on his progress with Grogu. The doubtful Guardians expressed his concerns that Grogu's progress was due to him gradually remembering his training on Coruscant, not necessarily his training so far with him. Ahsoka stated that sometimes the student teaches the master. Thompson noted Djarin's presence and Tano gave him a present meant for Grogu. Not sure whether Grogu had heart for the way of the Guardians, Thompson shared his thoughts. Fortunately, Tano knew the same mind of Thompson's father, seeing that same compassionate heart which she felt Anakin had for her and mentioned Jack's alikeness. Thompson asked what should be done and Tano told him to trust his instincts, wisdom she learned from her master. She then departed the planet and said the two would perhaps meet again and told The Energy be with him.[229]

Heeding the advice of Tano, Thompson decided to offer Grogu a final choice. In the stone hut, Thompson presented Grogu with Din Djarin's gift as well as the Lightblade of Master Yorin, which Thompson had kept. The first path was to accept the present from Djarin and be returned to him, however, by choosing attachment he would forsake the Guardians way. The second was to pick up Yorin's Lightblade and pledge himself to Jack's teachings to become his first Guardians Padawan. He told Grogu that to become a Guardians would take years and hard work, though at the end he could be a great Guardians. He then let Grogu choose.[229]

Grogu would choose the armor, thus leading Thompson to put him in his X-wing starfighter and instruct Artoo to pilot him to Tatooine to be reunited with Djarin.[230] Despite their time together being brief, Thompson's guidance had a significant impact on Grogu. After being asked about Grogu's impressive use of The Energy by fellow Mandalorian Bo-Katan Kryze, Djarin noted that his abilities were a result of Thompson's training.[231]

Some time after his meeting with Ahsoka Tano, the Nightsister Morgan Elsbeth recruited former Guardians Knight of the old Guardians Order Baylan Skoll, who was aware of the truth behind Thompson's father, to help locate Imperial Grand Admiral Thrawn. As Thompson had become prominent during the Imperial Civil War, Skoll became aware of his existence, and kept his name, alongside the names of other Freedom heroes, listed in a device on his gauntlet during the search for the planet Peridea in a distant galaxy.[232]

Recruiting his nephew

Though reluctant to take on another apprentice at first, when The Energy awakened in Thompson's nephew Ben Sullivan[4] and his mother Leah could see the dark side taking hold of her son, she entrusted the boy to her brother to help him find balance.[216] He agreed to train the 10-year-old Sullivan,[4] in 15 ABY.[234] Thompson took on the responsibilities of a Guardians Master by teaching his nephew. After Ben's fall to the dark side however, Jack would say that he was merely playing the role of Guardians Master as it was a title he had not formally earned.[216]

Thompson taught Sullivan the ways of the Guardians, in its many forms, under the tutelage and impressed on his pupil the importance of practicing discipline and control. Sullivan gave his best efforts to gain his Master's approval, although at times their familial bond caused him to address Thompson as uncle instead of Master.[7] Though he loved his nephew,[235] Thompson had committed his life to reestablishing the Guardians Order's teachings, which had virtually nothing to do with family ties; therefore, he taught Sullivan that detachment and distance were necessary to gaining the pure focus that every Guardians aspired to achieve.[7]

His nephew soon joined in exploring the galaxy for ancient Guardians lore,[4] and together they found holocrons from forgotten eras and ancient Guardians texts with arcane writings.[197] The two shared more than the same Thompson blood, for like him, Ben gained a strong devotion to the ways of the Guardians Order.[216] Thompson saw his nephew's raw power and believed he could pass on his strengths to him,[19] and he attempted to manage his progress through The Energy by having him exercise a level of restraint.[236] Sullivan was his strongest and most prized student,[20] and Thompson went on to place his hope for the Guardians Order's future within his nephew,[216] and despite knowing of the darkness within his pupil, the Guardians Master believed he was strong enough to keep it in check.[218]

Thompson was a kind master,[219] and Sullivan considered his uncle to be a strong and amazing teacher,[233] but Thompson made mistakes that culminated over time, protecting Ben from the dark truths of their family, much like his sister did before him, and inadvertently isolated Ben over the years of his training.[4] At the request of his sister, Jack never provided his nephew with the details of Anakin Thompson's fall to the dark side or Victor Darkwood's redemption and return to the light.[197]

During his training of his nephew, a powerful dark side-practitioner known as Snoke,[233] who was the Supreme Leader of a splinter group of the Union known as the First Order, and also secretly a puppet of the resurrected Emperor Paladin,[17] began influencing Ben from afar, clouding him with the dark side.[233] At some point Thompson had encountered Snoke and supposedly damaged him,[20] and Snoke was responsible for indirectly inspiring Jack to rebuild the Guardians Order, having tried to shatter his resolve.[218]

More apprentices

Around 16 ABY,[3] Thompson gathered disciples, young Energy-sensitive beings adrift with no way to harness their abilities as a result the Guardians Purge,[197] who went on to be his first students.[218] Thompson's new students numbered about a dozen besides his nephew,[210] including students Voe, Tai, and Hennix,[20] and he worked to teach them everything he had learned in his own training and about the light side of The Energy per Yorin's command.[237]

Thompson hoped that teaching the students in a real Guardians Temple would offer them a more traditional Guardians path than his own training under Benjamin Stone and Yorin. He kept temple's location on Ossus a secret known only to the members of the Order and his loyal droid Artoo.[218] Thompson stored the recovered Guardians knowledge and relics that he and Lor San Tekka had recovered within the Guardians Temple,[7] a decision which Sullivan disagreed, which he believed was just locked up and never used.[233]

With the small gathering of his new students looking to him as a spiritual guide and mentor for the Guardians arts, Thompson believed he could usher in a new era of enlightenment.[197] With his attention now split between several other apprentices, Thompson's nephew Ben Sullivan's focus was drawn away to dark Energy knowledge.[4] Teaching several Guardians techniques and traditions as telekinesis, meditation, and the use and construction of lightsabers,[225] Thompson's approach for guiding the young minds under his care was steeped in his own master Yorin's teachings passed on while he was on Dagobah, so would often be preaching about patience and balance.[197]

While also instilling the belief that all beings were equal within The Energy,[197] he gave lectures outside his temple, passing on ever more experience from training with Yorin on Dagobah as he taught the levitation of rocks through The Energy. During one such event, student Voe asked why Ben Sullivan was stronger, where Thompson compared The Energy to a door, that it would become easier to use the more she opened herself up to it. The Guardians Master also tutored Hennix in opening holocrons through The Energy, reminding the young Quarren to listen to the unique voices of each holocron, for each one was different based on their teachings.[225]

Thompson desired heavily to preserve the teachings of the Guardians Order, laboring to pass on the best of what Yorin and Benjamin taught him as Guardians mentors at his Guardians temple and to instill the core beliefs they embodied within the next generation. Additionally, Jack understood what Yorin experienced with himself on Dagobah as teacher, having the same unique frustrations with his own pupils; a master guiding a young unseasoned student that was convinced they were wasting time on pointless exercises, such as stacking stones.[197] At some point during his time as a Guardians Master, Thompson would face a group of IG-RM assassin droids alongside his nephew.[238]

Encountering the Knights of Ren

In 19 ABY, the year his nephew turned fourteen,[197] Thompson, Ben, and Lor San Tekka traveled to a Guardians outpost on Elphrona, a location from the days of the High Republic Era, hoping to find Guardians artifacts. Jack flew the ship and conversed with San Tekka, while Sullivan, without his master's knowledge, was communing with Snoke. Thompson was initially skeptical of a Guardians presence so far from the core, but San Tekka assured him that with his expertise he had recognized the description from his contact as a Guardians Outpost, and explained that the Guardians's activity was greatly expanded throughout the galaxy during the High Republic Era. Meanwhile, Sullivan telepathically communicated with Snoke, expressing that he was tired of missions to find artifacts with Thompson that he would just lock away in the temple and never use. Sullivan admitted that Thompson was an amazing teacher, but was frustrated that he never wanted Sullivan to use any of the things he had taught him, which Snoke claimed was because Thompson viewed Sullivan as a child and had not realized his true capabilities. Thompson then told Sullivan that they would possibly find insights into the old Guardians, and added that they could find interesting weapons, as he knew that Sullivan was interested in such subjects, which San Tekka affirmed. Thompson told Sullivan he was glad he came along, and Sullivan said he shared the sentiment.[233]

When they landed, Thompson was impressed by the sight of the outpost. He cautioned his apprentice to careful and warned that such a place was likely to be rigged with traps, recalling a time he found a Lightblade in a similar place that turned out to be a trap and nearly killed him. Inside, they found a treasure trove that Thompson judged so impressive he thought it may be better than Jocasta Nu's cache, including many Guardians holocrons and a Lightblade rifle. Thompson was excited by the possibility of learning many new things. As the three perused the outpost, instantly, both Thompson and Sullivan felt a cold presence within the structure. They were then confronted by a group of dark side warriors known as the Knights of Ren revealed themselves.[233]

Their master, "Ren," declared themselves, and claimed ownership of all within the outpost and that they way they took it was up to Thompson. Thompson countered, shooting back that all within the outpost, due it being a Guardians location, belonged to the Order; all the holocrons and relics belonged to Jack, for he was the last Guardians Knight even though he would not be for long. Thompson told the Knights he could sense the dark side in them and would never let them have the artifacts. When Ren insulted him, Sullivan stepped forward and declared that his master was a legend. Thompson ignited his saber and instructed San Tekka to hang back and Sullivan to protect San Tekka, while handled the threat, assuring Sullivan he could do it even thought there were seven of them. Ren tried to taunt him, but Thompson just taunted him back, angering him and moving him to order the Knights to attack. As Sullivan watched on and called out to him, Thompson engaged the knights, not intending to kill any of them, and managed to overpower them quite easily despite their numbers, which he explained as him not being alone because he had the powerful ally that was The Energy by his side. He criticized them for their crude use of The Energy, saying they wielded the dark side like a hammer, contrasted by his blade-like precision with the light side. He eventually used The Energy to destroy the Knights' weapons and proceeded to prepare himself for a duel with Ren, who wielded a red Lightblade. Ren informed Thompson that he activated a kill switch on his Lightblade that would detonate the room if it left his hand. Thompson warned that move might not go the way Ren expected, which Ren agreed with. Realizing that Thompson's power was far superior to their own, Ren withdrew his knights and left the planet. However, before he left, Ren removed his helmet and warned Thompson about the strong presence of the dark side within Sullivan, before offering the boy chance, if he chose, to seek them out if decided he wanted another path.[233]

Ultimately, the structure proved to be a source of more knowledge for Thompson,[219] for he recovered the holocrons and other relics inside, leaving the outpost empty,[225] but his nephew was prompted with the dark side of The Energy.[219]

Quest for Exegol

Visions and voices

As his first disciples grew older[20] and new younglings came into the New Guardians Order's care, Thompson had a Twi'lek named Enyo help him as one of the younglings' instructors.[7]

In 21 ABY,[239] Thompson started feeling an increasing darkness in The Energy and having dark visions that frequently came to him in meditation. One day, Lor San Tekka, who was the only person Thompson told about the visions, visited Thompson in his temple on Ossus, his old friend's arrival heralded to Thompson by Ben Sullivan. San Tekka greeted Thompson and Sullivan, but once the latter was gone, asked Thompson about the visions and whether or not they had become stronger or changed. Thompson asked San Tekka to help him understand the visions and learn what the place in them was by standing vigil over him while he accessed the vision and described it. San Tekka was hesitant, but agreed, and Thompson thus began the meditation.[7]

Much quicker than his other meditations, Thompson found himself in an empty dark void but felt an all-consuming presence within it which he had only thought he had sensed before. Then the vision shifted and he found himself standing on the ancient hidden Shadow Knights world of Exegol, though he did not know that was what he was seeing. Eventually the vision broke, and Thompson explained to San Tekka what he had seen. San Tekka wondered if the vision was just a general representation of lingering darkness or an influence from an artifact, but Thompson still believed he was having the vision for a reason. Thompson asked San Tekka the reason he had come to Ossus in the first place, and he explained that there was an archaeological dig on the planet Yoturba that he wanted to check out with Thompson, convincing him to leave the temple by saying that it would be good for Sullivan to learn responsibilities while he was gone.[7]

Yoturba

Thompson and San Tekka arrived at the archeological expedition on Yorturba that was being conducted by the Lerct Historical Institute and were greeted by archaeologist Dr. Beaumont Kin, who informed them that the dig site was disappointing and they had not found a Guardians temple. Thompson still carried a feeling that he was there for a reason despite this, and he would be proven correct when an archaeologist named Tee Mairi discovered a Shadow Knights holocron at the site. Thompson recognized it immediately, and San Tekka recognized the writing on it as the Shadow Knights's mirrored Aurebesh. Thompson still was not convinced that it was what he had sensed there, and would be again proved correct when he heard screaming echoing throughout the site. Archeologist Mico Haswell had discovered a Shadow Knights kyber crystal and had his mind influenced by it, causing him to uncontrollably lash out and cause destruction with The Energy. Jack restrained him with The Energy, but in reaching out to him once again had a vision of Exegol that Haswell was now present in. Haswell leapt toward Thompson in the vison and moved in a way that Thompson recognized to be inhuman and even reptilian. Then Thompson was on Yoturba again and managed to restrain the man with The Energy again, calling a team of medics to sedate him via an injection.[7]

Haswell was once again in control of himself, and Thompson questioned him about what happened, though the man was not able to remember much. He did tell Thompson that he saw a dry, dark place filled with lightning and felt a shadow-like sentient presence there. Once Haswell passed out, Thompson discovered the bled kyber crystal in his hand, and confiscated it. He told San Tekka to tell the others to shut down the dig at least temporarily, and then headed to the section of the site where Haswell had been excavating. He discovered that the chamber was actually a crashed ship, and discovered more bled kyber crystals within it, that like the one Haswell had been holding, were the wrong size and shape for a Lightblade. This and his experience on the planet confirmed for Thompson that something dark and evil was coming, deciding to leave San Tekka there to look over the dig site while he went to Tython in search of answers.[7]

Ghosts of the past

Thompson traveled to Tython and visited the ruined Guardians temple known as The Energy-henge, where he sat on the seeing stone and began to study the Shadow Knights Holocron and the kyber crystals and perform research through his many ancient tomes and personal notes. In a book that was a catalog of the Eeshaypher Collection, Thompson discovered an entry about a Shadow Knights wayfinder, which he found remarkably similar in appearance and utility to the Shadow Knights holocron. The book stated that the wayfinders contained navigational data to an ancient Shadow Knights world called Ixigul—or in Thompson's translation: Exegol. Thompson gleaned from both the catalog and his other research that Exegol was meant to be a place of power and eternal life, and that its secret was so important that only two Shadow Knights wayfinders were ever made. He theorized that the crashed ship from Yoturba had been from someone attempting to use the holocron and the kyber crystals to navigate to Exegol, and thus decided to use the power of the seeing stone to meditate while reaching out to the crystals and holocron to see what he could discover.[7]

During his meditation, Thompson once again had a vision of Exegol, but this time if felt like he was truly on the planet, causing him to wonder if the information stored in the holocron had allowed the seeing stone to actually transport him to the planet. Thompson was then approached by nine Shadow Knights wraiths on the planet who ignited red lightsabers and began to attack him. Thompson could block their lightsabers with his own, but could not attack the wraiths themselves, his blade and his body merely passing right through them. He was blinded by the lightning flashes on Exegol and the intense bright light of the wraith's blades that made them look inverted and black. Thompson started struggling to fight them more and more until all nine wraiths eventually went in for one coordinated strike, but before they could a blue glow in the shape of a person holding a Lightblade appeared in from of Thompson and pushed them away. Thompson realized he was being assisted by a Energy-spirit with a blue-bladed Lightblade and initially thought was Stone, but when the figure lowered his hood, Thompson learned that it was his father, Anakin Thompson. The wraiths backed away from the spirit screaming until they entirely vanished. Anakin Thompson then reached out his hand to Jack, who took it and then found himself back on Tython as if he had never left.[7]

Jack found that many of his books were burning and the holocron was completely destroyed, putting them out before speaking to Anakin. Anakin's figure flashed in and out and shifted between him as a young Guardians and him as the old man Jack had seen behind the mask of Victor Darkwood. Jack noticed that he seemed to be in pain and afraid. Anakin explained that there was a disturbance in The Energy that was a shadow from an ancient time. He also clarified that the seeing stone had sent a part of Jack to Exegol and that it had taken everything in him to bring him back. Anakin reminded Jack that he could divert the path of The Energy if he needed to, and that no matter how dark things seemed he was never alone as long as he allowed The Energy to be his guide. Anakin then disappeared and left Jack alone until he heard the sound of a ship flying overhead and landing. It was Lando Calrissian, in the Lady Luck, who greeted Jack, but immediately told him that he needed to leave and that it was too dangerous for him to be there. But Calrissian told him that Lor San Tekka had told him where he was because Calrissian had information about the Shadow Knights, and also believed they were back.[7]

Calrissian's mission

Calrissian brought Thompson onto his ship and explained that overheard an assassin named Ochi of Bestoon who had become inebriated at a remote bar bragging that he had been sent by a Shadow Knights remnant to kidnap a girl from her parents. Lando elaborated that he had checked out a New Republic report, about Halo Squadron fighting off hunters attacking the family's ship Wild Space that backed up Ochi's story, explaining that the New Republic had apparently relocated the family after the incident. He then displayed a hologram of a hex charm that the family had been carrying as proof that they were being hunted by the Shadow Knights. Thompson recognized it immediately as a symbol of the Shadow Knights and decoded mirrored Aurebesh on it to be a Shadow Knights saying.[7]

Calrissian expressed his worry over Ochi's implication that the Shadow Knights were still out there pulling strings, and wondered why they would want that family and go through the trouble of kidnapping the girl. Thompson told Calrissian about his visions of Exegol, and told him that the way to the planet was hidden in a Shadow Knights wayfinder. Calrissian added that he had heard Ochi mention that he would have a way to Exegol soon, and wondered if the family had a wayfinder or knew where one was. But unbeknownst to Thompson and Calrissian, the father of the family was a strand-cast of the late Emperor Paladin named Dathan, and the girl, his daughter named Rey, was thus Paladin's genetic granddaughter.[7]

Calrissian decided that he and Thompson needed to go together to find the family and stop the hunter from getting to them, urging Thompson to get ahead of the shadow that he had sensed before it was too late. Thompson was hesitant to bring Calrissian along, but agreed to partially because he was aware of Calrissian's daughter's kidnapping and his friend's desperate hope that investigating this kidnapping could lead to her. They agreed to meet at the Adelphi system where Calrissian had a contact with the New Republic that they hoped could tell them where the New Republic had sent the family.[7]

Nightside

Thompson and Calrissian met with the latter's contact, Shriv Suurgav, in the New Republic staging outpost over the planet Adelphi. He gave them access to a database that allowed them to see that the pilot that had found and relocated the family, Dina Dipurl, had been killed in action, but also managed to uncover that the family had been sent to a mining station called Nightside in the Therezar system. Thompson and Calrissian traveled to the station with the hope of making contact with Calrissian's old acquaintance Zargo Anaximander. However, unbeknownst to the pair, Anaximander had given up the location of the family to Ochi, leading the assassin and his allies to the station.[7]

Using a clearance code that Anaximander had given Calrissian years earlier, Thompson and Calrissian were able to land their ship in the VIP hangar of Nightside. They located the family's ship but discovered that it was empty. Using The Energy, Thompson located the family and sensed that they were aboard Anaximander's personal yacht Goldstone. Artoo then tapped into the station's surveillance systems and discovered from recordings that Dathan and Miramir were planning to escape with their daughter Rey. Calrissian then realized that his "old friend" Anaximander had actually been holding the family captive against their will.[7]

Before Thompson and Calrissian can reach the family, the station under attack by the Droid Crush Pirates of Bestoon, who, even though they were working for Ochi, ignored Ochi's orders for a clean extraction of the family and instead performed a raid on Nightside. Thompson was able to sense in The Energy that the attackers were the same people pursuing the family. During the attack, Thompson and Calrissian split up, Thompson tasking Calrissian with helping the family escape while he took on the assailants.[7]

Left alone, Thompson used The Energy to track a Shadow Knights presence that he felt, ending up in a cable train station. A masked Pantoran woman then landed there in a customized imperial TIE Defender, and when she exited it, Thompson noticed that she appeared to be a Shadow Knights and was wielding the ancient lost Lightblade of Victor Noctyss. The two began to duel, the woman ordering Thompson to hand her the shards of the bled Kyber crystal from Yoturba. Realizing that she was possessed by a Shadow Knights spirit, Thompson offered to help free her while warning that he would defend himself. Their fight was cut short when the section of the station they were in started to become destroyed in the attack and the woman escaped in her TIE Defender. Thompson was saved by Calrissian in the Lady Luck, who informed Thompson that he had helped the family escape the station in Anaximander's personal Yacht the Goldstone.[7]

Torrenoteer Minor

Thompson and Calrissian followed the Goldstone via transmissions aboard the Lady Luck, Thompson telling Thompson about his encounter with the Shadow Knights acolyte on Nightside. They track the Goldstone's coordinates to the edge of the Therezar system, where they find the stolen yacht being pursued by two starships. Before the Goldstone jumped into hyperspace, Artoo was able to deduce that the family was fleeing to the Torrenoteer system. Following the trail of the Goldstone, Thompson and Calrissian land Lady Luck on the desert world of Torrenoteer Minor, the two continuing their search for the family on two Stacker Polaris Stormwolf speeder bikes. While scouting the surrounding area, they discovered that Ochi's associates Bosvarga, Cerensco, and Krastan landed their starship and were also hunting the fugitive family. When the bounty hunters split into two parties, Thompson and Calrissian also decided to split up in order to track the hunters.[7]

While riding his speeder bike through a ravine system, Thompson was ambushed by the two bounty hunters, also riding speeder bikes. Thompson deflected their blaster bolts of using his Lightblade, and hit one bolt back at one of the hunters, causing the hunter's speeder bike to crash in a ravine. Thompson chased the second speeder over a lake, once again deflecting the hunter's blaster bolts, causing the hunter and his speeder bike to fall in the lake by hitting the bolts back at him. Thompson then approached the Weequay hunter in his wrecked vehicle and attempted to question him, but he fought Thompson instead and subsequently died from being impaled by his speeder bike's steering vane. While examining the hunter's bike, Thompson discovered a message indicating that the hunter's associates believe they have found something "nice and juicy."[7]

Heading to coordinates transmitted by the hunter's associates, Thompson found that the Gran hunter Krastan had captured Lando and was holding him in the prisoner clamp of a CAP-2 walker. Thompson fought Krastan with his Lightblade, killing him by defecting his own blaster bolts back at him. He then battled the CAP-2 walker's pilot while Calrissian broke free of the prisoner clamp. Thompson sliced the CAP-2's left leg with his Lightblade, causing the machine to stumble, before using The Energy to throw the machine to the ground. After taking out the pilot, Thompson rejoined and attended to Lando, who explained that the hunters had followed him to the Goldstone but that the ship had fled before they could breach it. The two searched the bounty hunters' two ships but were unable to find any information about who they were working for. While attempting to access one of the ship's navigation computers, Thompson inadvertently activated a self-destruct mechanism that destroyed the ship's systems, preventing them from tracking down the family's ship. Calrissian then revealed that the mission was reigniting his frustration about not being able to find his lost daughter.[7]

Polaar

Thompson had begun to suspect that the woman he encountered was related to a dark side cult called the Acolytes of the Beyond that he had fought before. Thompson and Calrissian used his friend Lina Graf's ship the Worm Herald to travel to the planet Polaar to get information from Komat, a woman who was formerly part of the Acolytes of the Beyond who collected Shadow Knights artifacts like the Lightblade of Victor Noctyss. Thompson also believed that she would have the ability to track the Goldstone for them. When Thompson had previously encountered the Acolytes of the Beyond, he had met her and had helped her leave the cult. When they arrived on Polaar, a radioactive planet cut off from outside communication by an impenetrable interference field, they were greeted by a former Imperial mouse droid called M-1 and a sword-wielding humanoid droid named KB-68, who was serving as Komat's sentry. Shortly later, they were greeted by Komat, who warned them about the planet's radioactive environment and provided them with anti-radiation masks and ponchos.[7]

Agreeing to help them if they helped her in turn, Komat took them to a wrecked Mon Calamari cruiser that crashed on Polaar during the Imperial Civil War, explaining that with the shields overloaded, the ship's fusion core had melted down, contaminating the planet with distronic radiation that rendered it uninhabitable for most lifeforms. Thompson and Calrissian help Komat repair her particle capture grid, which she used to harvest radiation that KB-68 sold for supplies in the Pirsen system. In return, Komat revealed that the woman Thompson had encountered was named Kiza, and had once been her friend. She explained that Kiza had worked directly for Yupe Tashu, the leader Acolytes of the Beyond and one of the Emperor's advisors, who had gifted her the Mask of Viceroy Exim Panshard which contained the preserved consciousness of the ancient Shadow Knights lord Exim Panshard and allowed him to take over her when she wore it. Thompson revealed that they believed she was working with Ochi to hunt down a fugitive family in order to obtain a wayfinder. Explaining that two Shadow Knights wayfinders were made as navigational guides to Exegol, the hidden Shadow Knights world, Komat became determined to help Thompson and Calrissian stop Kiza and keep her from reaching Exegol.[7]

Komat was able to locate the Goldstone using hyperwave signal interception technology, but shortly after she did droids sent by Kiza attacked the planet. Thompson and Calrissian join Komat, KB-68, and Komat's feline pet targon Sekhmet in battling the droids. Thompson and the others managed to defeat them, and after being brought one of the droid's heads by M-1, they discovered that the droids contained bled kyber crystals that were also pieces of the large broken one Thompson had found on Yoturba. Thompson decided that he would use the droids' navigational data to locate Kiza and make sure that she and subsequently Exim Panshard did not make it to Exegol, and thus make sure the Shadow Knights did not return. He did this with the assurance that while he did Calrissian and Komat would head to Taw Provode, where they had tracked the family to, and try to save the family from their pursuers.[7]

Using coordinates retrieved from the droid lander's navigation computer, Thompson and Artoo located Kiza by tracking the attackers' origin to an asteroid at the edge of the Red Honeycomb Zone. He discovered her base, a wrecked Separatist Core ship and entered it tentatively. He discovered a droid forge and a meditation chamber with the Mask of Exim Panshard sitting on a plinth inside, but when he looked at it was overwhelmed with all consuming vision's of Panshard's life, including his acts of mass murder committed against his people in order to drain his life Energy in an attempt to live forever. When Kiza arrived, Thompson attempted to try and appeal to her and choose a good path, with the woman even clearly expressing regret. He insisted that the mask was using her and she did not have to listen to it, but Kiza explained that it had promised her rebirth on Exegol, which Thompson told her was a lie. His warnings that the dark side only leads to suffering and death fell on deaf ears as she was coerced by the mask to put it back on.[7]

Kiza engaged Thompson in Lightblade combat, fueled by the knowledge of Panshard. Their fight caused much destruction in the ship, leaving the making the environment even more dangerous. While fighting, Kiza used The Energy in attempt to pour hot molten metal onto Thompson, but the Guardians Master used The Energy to escape the ensuing molten fire in the droid foundry. However, molten material began to spread everywhere melt the ship. Thompson attempted one last time to reach Kiza, pleading with her to leave it behind and join him. Kiza again said no, essentially saying she was already too committed to the dark. The core ship collapsed and rubble fell on top of and killed Kiza, much to Thompson's dismay.[7]

Thompson then retrieved several ancient texts from the meditation chamber but was unable to find Panshard's mask. He returned to the Worm Herald and discovered a holomessage from Calrissian asking for him to go to Taw Provode as quick as he could to help with their situation with the family and Ochi of Bestoon there. But unbeknownst to Thompson, Panshard's mask survived, and as he departed some of Kiza's droids followed programming to reforge the mask with melted bled kyber crystals, before placing the mask on Kiza's dead body, allowing Panshard to live again by possessing and reanimating her body.[7]

Clue to Exegol

Thompson arrived on Taw Provode just as Calrissian and Komat had been discovered and captured by Ochi of Bestoon and his subordinate Corsec forces. Thompson faced off with Ochi, and was surprised that his Lightblade did not cut through Ochi's dagger. The power of Ochi's dagger allowed him to hold himself off against the Guardians for some time until Thompson managed to use his Lightblade to ignite a fuels reserve and trigger a massive explosion. Panshard appeared in his TIE Defender and started shooting at Thompson and the others, leading Thompson to realize that the mask was not really destroyed and the ancient Shadow Knights Lord lived on. Thompson and Komat decided to chase him into space, while Calrissian and Artoo went after the family.[7]

Thompson and Komat followed Panshard to Taw Provode's solitary moon, using the Worm Herald to shoot his TIE Defender down and strand him on the moon. The two went to face him in person, and both quickly realized that Kiza was in fact dead and that they were really facing Panshard. Panshard told Thompson to tell him how to get to Exegol or be killed, but Thompson and Komat refused and instead faced off with their lightsabers. Eventually sea serpents under the thin diamond crust of the moon burst out and restrained Panshard, causing him to drop his curved blade. Thompson picked it up and then called the mask to him with The Energy before slicing it into four pieces with the two lightsabers and destroying Panshard for good.[7]

Thompson was informed by Calrissian that he had found the bodies of the parents of the family, but that the girl was still missing. Thompson, Calrissian, Artoo, and Komat traveled to Neftali, where they buried them and honored their lives. Thompson and Calrissian worried that Ochi had successfully captured the girl and taken her to Exegol, but they could not be sure, and decided to keep looking for Ochi.[7]

After Calrissian took a time to recover by working with Komat at her homestead, Thompson and Calrissian eventually tracked Ochi to Pasaana where they found his ship but no Ochi. They did find a sheet of old-looking paper with a wayfinder drawn on it that proved that Ochi had had a clue to a wayfinder,[7] but other than that, the trail went cold.[17] Thompson then returned to Ossus to continue running his Guardians academy and left Calrissian on Pasanna, who decided to use the planet as his base to look for both the lost girl and his daughter. Thompson stored the pieces of bled kyber crystal and the shards of Exim Panshard's mask in the temple with other artifacts.[7] Thompson would chronicle his efforts to trace the disturbance in The Energy and find the Shadow Knights Wayfinder in the Rammahgon throughout his explorations in the galaxy.[4]

Telling a story

During Ben's training on Ossus, Thompson asked his nephew to accompany him on a walk through the temple grounds. As they walked, Thompson told Sullivan that he was almost ready to undergo the Guardians Trials, the completion of which would mark the end of their time together as Master and apprentice.[240]

According to Thompson, Sullivan would no longer have to listen to a master since he would be free to make his own decisions as a fully-trained Guardians Knight. In addition, Thompson acknowledged Sullivan's potential to become a future leader in the Guardians Order that he was working to restore, given his nephew's status as a Thompson, his strength in The Energy, and the fact that he stood poised to be among the first in his generation to complete his instruction in the Guardians arts. Having faced his own challenges as a leader, Thompson wished to tell his apprentice a story about an incident from his past, its purpose to serve as a lesson in making difficult decisions.[240]

Thompson's story centered on his discovery and subsequent use of the Grim Rose, an ancient weapon that he discovered upon returning to the Living Sea on the planet Gazian. Sullivan learned that Thompson had considered using the Grim Rose to assassinate Emperor Paladin; using a sample of Paladin's blood, the weapon could be used to target and kill the Emperor. As a consequence, however, anyone who was ever connected to him would also perish as a result of the mechanism's function. Sullivan theorized that the weapon had the capacity to destroy the Shadow Knights. Being aware of Darkwood's connection to Paladin, Sullivan was certain that the weapon would have scanned Darkwood as well, ultimately resulting in the deaths of both Shadow Knights Lords. Thompson revealed that while he had considered that possibility, he was still reluctant to allow the Grim Rose to carry out its programming, having begun to think that he could redeem Darkwood. By the end of the story, Thompson revealed that the lesson behind the story was "to not defeat evil by becoming evil." Sullivan questioned if it was a true story or simply a parable, to which Thompson assured him that it was all true.[240]

Fallen apprentice

Ben Sullivan's betrayal

One night in 28 ABY,[241] Thompson, sensing the darkness within his nephew Ben growing in glimpses during his training, decided to probe Sullivan's mind as he lay asleep. He learned that Snoke had turned his apprentice's heart to the dark side,[19] and experienced a brief dark vision[4] that Sullivan would bring nothing but death and destruction[19] and leave Thompson's loved ones in anguish. Despite the love for his nephew,[235] in a flash of defensive instinct,[4] Thompson thought he could stop that future and for a brief moment of pure instinct he raised his Lightblade to destroy him, believing that it was the only way to halt his descent into darkness and prevent the end of everything he loved. This instinct was gone almost the second that it had come, and Thompson was immediately filled with shame.[19]

Thompson had failed to correctly view that his nephew's mind was still conflicted, and his choice not yet made between light and darkness.[219] From Rey's point of view, Thompson's failure as not only Sullivan's Guardians Master, but his uncle and family, had been completed when this occurred.[19]

Sullivan had already awoken to see his uncle standing over him with his Lightblade raised. Confused and angry, Sullivan pulled his own Lightblade to him in self-defense and attacked Thompson, using The Energy to pull down the hut on top of him.[19] Sullivan emerged from the hut and screamed toward the temple, asking Thompson why he had done it, and in his anger bringing down a lightning strike with The Energy that destroyed the temple and set it on fire.[3][20] When Thompson came around, he emerged from the rubble of the hut to find his temple destroyed, with his other apprentices either killed or vanished along with Sullivan. This effectively left Thompson the last of the Guardians once more[19] as he was after the death of his own master, Yorin, decades earlier.[12]

Three of Thompson's apprentices, Tai, Hennix, and Voe, eventually returned to the temple while it burned and were not able to sense his presence, which they normally felt overwhelmingly in The Energy. When Sullivan claimed that Thompson had tried to kill him and that he was dead, they did not believe the former, but did believe the latter because they could not sense their master's presence. Sullivan claimed that he was stronger than Thompson when it mattered and left the planet, the three pursuing Sullivan with the hope of avenging Thompson. When Sullivan considered leaving to go to his mother in the aftermath, he heard the voice of Snoke in his head perpetuating his belief that Thompson had tried to kill him, as he and Sullivan had feared it before, claiming that Thompson was "small" and feared Sullivan and his strength. Snoke continued to speak to Sullivan, blaming Thompson for everything that had happened and claiming he chose it, and Sullivan decided to go to Snoke instead of his mother. On the Amaxine Space Station, with Snoke, Sullivan noticed the man's appearance and asked him what Thompson had done to him, but Snoke redirected the conversation to what Thompson had done to Sullivan. Sullivan tried to claim it did not matter because he had killed the Guardians Master, but Snoke warned that Thompson was not dead and would not die so easily, though agreed to stop talking about Thompson because he had "stolen" too much of his life already.[20] Sullivan eventually became the apprentice of Snoke and took the new name "Kylo Ren," becoming the leader of a group of dark side warriors known as the Knights of Ren.[8]

Left deeply affected and mentally broken by what he believed to be his ultimate failure, Thompson lost faith in The Energy and the vision of the Guardians for having failed to rebuild the Guardians Order. Thompson believed he had failed to protect his students, and also failed to save his own flesh and blood from his family's dark legacy. So Thompson fled into self-imposed exile, vanishing from the galaxy at-large without a trace. No one knew for sure where he went, but those closest to him believed he went off in search of the first Guardians Temple.[8]

To those who knew what Thompson was seeking, there remained a way to find him: after destroying the Guardians, the Union had begun to seek out the thousands of Guardians temples scattered across the galaxy, with the aim to prevent anyone from discovering the lost secrets of the Order. As such, Imperial authorities discovered the locations of some of the first Guardians Temples. The partial map remained within the archives of the Union. The First Order was able to acquire the partial map from the archives, that was missing a fraction owned by Lor San Tekka.[242] As he had downloaded Imperial Worm maps during his time on the first Death Worm,[243] Artoo, Thompson's faithful astromech, was able to recover the contents of the map.[242]

Kylo Ren's vision

Later, while training Kylo Ren with an exercise in which he forced Ren to stop himself from falling, Snoke informed the boy that Thompson would have caught him if he had not caught himself, and would have thus held him back. Snoke then took Ren to the Cave of Evil on Dagobah, where Ren could sense that Thompson had been there, Snoke revealing that he had trained there before becoming one of the most formidable Guardians. Ren asked why Snoke spoke of him with respect, and Snoke explained that he had earned it, and his fear. Ren tried to claim that Thompson was weak, but Snoke refuted this, insisting that Thompson was only misguided and that he would have taken over the galaxy a long time before if Thompson had been his apprentice.[244]

Ren then entered the cave and found himself facing an apparition of Thompson, who insisted that he did not want to fight. Ren fought him anyway, his red crossguard Lightblade clashing against Thompson's green. Snoke egged Ren on and validated his hatred as he fought his old master, insisting that Thompson had feared what Ren would become, would have murdered him in his sleep, and was representative of the light side holding him back. Screaming as Snoke told him to strike, Ren sliced through Thompson's body, leaving the appearance of his dead body lying on the ground of the cave. While staring at the corpse, Ren declared himself ready.[244]

Exile on Ahch-To

Shutting himself off from The Energy

The Guardians Master fled to the location of the Temple—on an aquatic planet named Ahch-To—that he discovered by studying the spread of uneti saplings.[218] Having failed in bringing back the Guardians Order, Thompson intended nothing more than to live out the rest of his days there in reclusive exile[237] and had pledged to protect the hidden world and its many secrets.[245] Taking the relics he had uncovered with him, the hermit shut himself off from The Energy,[237] consequently ceasing the guidance of Benjamin Stone, Yorin, and Anakin Thompson's spirits.[216] It had taken years for him to learn the discipline of blocking The Energy out, and he considered the fact that it was harder to do so than to feel it and let it in a dark secret that was never taught. He believed The Energy and the Guardians had many secrets and that not even the greatest and in his opinion arrogant Guardians Masters could understand all of it. Even though occasionally his greatest efforts would not be enough and The Energy would reach him anyway, he felt as though he had achieved true solitude in closing himself off.[246]

Among the relics Jack had collected and brought into his exile were the sacred Guardians texts and the Guardians Crusader pendant.[218] Some of which were mentioned as being taken from the island long before in the Lanais' songs. The Caretakers tolerated Thompson's presence, though he had learned the Lanai's language and ways, insisted on doing most of their duties to care for him himself, and had appeared each month on the Festival of Return, something which puzzled Alcida-Auka.[247]

Though absent from Imperial affairs, Thompson's story was nonetheless a thing of myth and legend in certain corners of the galaxy. His exploits in the Imperial Civil War were known to the Resistance, a band of freedom fighters led by now-General Organa to combat the rise of the First Order. Resistance fighter pilot Jessika Pava learned that See-Threepio, who also served in the Resistance, knew Thompson and asked the droid to tell her a story about his former master. Threepio regaled her with the tale of Thompson's encounter with Sarco Plank on Devaron, and how Thompson kept his promise to Farnay and indeed returned to Devaron.[48] Members of the First Order, however, had a less-than-ideal view of the Guardians Master; the First Order taught their followers, including stormtroopers like FN-2187, that Thompson was an enemy who helped destroy the Union. Despite the knowledge about Thompson, the mystical elements of his life, as well as the Guardians in general, were seen as mythic rather than historic.[248]

Prior to Rey's arrival on Ahch-To, Jack Thompson had a dream while sleeping in his hut. In his dream, he saw the life he would never have, one in which he never left Tatooine and remained in the moisture farming business with his uncle, marrying Camie Marstrap and allowing the Imperial Union to shatter the Freedom Alliance and destroy more planets with the Death Worm. Jack eventually awoke, trying to shake away his dream, and left his hut. He was still astonished at the oceans of Ahch-To, which he attributed to growing up in the deserts of Tatooine. As he reached a meadow overlooking the sea, he was still unable to shake the dream away, believing it was not an ordinary dream, but The Energy at work.[247] Also shortly before Rey arrived, one of the moments where The Energy demanded to be heard and got through despite Thompson's block occurred. In that moment, the old Guardians Master undeniably felt that The Energy was telling him that someone had found him.[246]

Thompson is found

In 34 ABY, Organa sent Resistance fighter Poe Dameron,[8] the son of Shara Bey and Kes Dameron,[210] to Jakku to find San Tekka and recover the map to Thompson's alleged location, as she hoped her brother would return and help in the fight against the dark side. The First Order also learned of the map and sought it so they could destroy the last Guardians. Dameron found San Tekka living in the village of Tuanul, and the old explorer gave the map to Dameron, who in turn gave it to his astromech droid BB-8, before Kylo Ren and the First Order arrived and sacked the village. Ren killed San Tekka and took Dameron into custody in the hopes of finding the map, while BB-8 came into the possession of a Jakku scavenger named Rey. Rey believed that Thompson was only a myth, but she too possessed strength in The Energy that she had not yet discovered. Rey teamed with FN-2187, an ex-Stormtrooper who took the name Finn after defecting from the First Order, and, with the help of Hank Sullivan and Chester, sought to return the map fragment to the Resistance.[8]

After Rey discovered the Lightblade that once belonged to both Anakin and Jack in the pirate castle belonging to Maz Kanata on Takodana, a thousand year-old being who had knowledge of The Energy, she began to unlock her own Energy abilities. After being captured by Ren on Takodana and briefly interrogated onboard Starkiller Base in an attempt by Ren to analyze Rey's thoughts to find the path to Thompson, BB-8's map fragment was soon combined with Artoo's, and together they provided a complete map to Ahch-To. Accompanied by Chester, Rey flew the Millennium Falcon to Ahch-To and found the old Guardians Master on an island.[8]

When Rey arrived on the island, Thompson was standing up top the saddle, summoning the will to try to torch the tree library again having just failed to do so.[247] While standing there, Thompson was reflecting that his efforts to block The Energy from him was impossible as it always demanded to be heard. But in that moment, the old Guardians Master felt that someone had found him, turning around to see who was, discovering Rey.[246] After making her way to Thompson, Rey presented him with his father's old weapon as a symbol of the only hope that the galaxy had left.[242]

Confronted with the past

After taking his old Lightblade and looking it over, Thompson,[19] emotion roiled over his face,[4] hoping to lose the blade once again,[36] tossed it behind him and left the plateau, with Rey following close behind him. Despite her repeated attempts, Thompson declined to help her or the Resistance, even though he knew that the girl had been sent by his sister Leah.[19] Secluding himself in his hut, Thompson thought to himself that he did not expect that the person who found him would be a child with what he considered a worthless antique and delusions of grandeur.[246]

Later, Jack was folding his old clothes then heard Rey trying to reach out to him again by knocking on his door but he told her to go away. However, Jack was surprised when his door broke open but was even more surprised to see his old friend Chester was with her. Hearing how they got to where he was with the Millennium Falcon, Jack asked where Hank was.[19] Rey and Chester told him that his old friend and brother-in-law was dead, murdered by Kylo Ren, which stunned Thompson. Feeling regret for not having sensed his brother-in-law's death because he had disconnected himself from The Energy, Jack was comforted by the Wookiee, who hugged him, before Thompson comforted Chester back.[246]

While hiking the island with a backpack, Thompson noticed the sacred uneti tree on the island and decided to go inside it. There he found Rey,[246] who had discovered several original texts of Guardians teachings hidden there. Thompson learned that she had The Energy within her and sought assistance to understand it and her place in the galaxy. Thompson refused to teach her revealed that he had come to the place where the Guardians started in order to die, and voiced his belief that it was time for the Guardians Order to die.[19]

The next night, the former Guardians Master slipped into the Millennium Falcon and recalled the adventures he had experienced.
